This "Newton/AM" method could be powerful and novel. Let's formalize:
Input p length n (probabilities). Let S = sum p. Let L=power2 >=2n-1. We want minimize max q. For each iteration:
- q = ifft(fft(p)^2)[:2n-1].
- M = max q / maybe desired upper M_target.
- Target q_t: we can choose a function of q: q_t = clip(q, max=M_target, min=0). To preserve sum, after clipping top to M_target, the sum q_t = S^2 - excess. To guide toward sum S^2, we can distribute excess to all q_t proportionally to some low mask: q_t = q_t + excess * w / sum w, where w=1 for q_k<M_target or q_k< M_target. Simpler: q_t = q - alpha*(q-M_target)_+, with alpha in [0,1]. If alpha=1, sum reduces by excess; we can then normalize q_t to sum S^2 by scaling (which may raise peaks slightly). Or use q_t = min(q, M_target) + (S^2 - sum min(q, M)) * flat_weight/sum flat_weight. This ensures sum constant and no above M except maybe if redistributed? For low coefficients, adding can still keep below M if enough room: total room = ∑(M - q_k) over low. Excess =∑(q_k-M) high. At target feasible, room≥excess. If not, can scale. Setting q_t=M for high and q_t=low+excess*room? A simple water-filling: target distribution of mass S^2 with upper cap M, minimizing L2 distance to q? Or just water level: q_t = min(q, M) + η, where η chosen to make sum S^2, but if q_k already near M, adding η may exceed M. We can cap: q_t = min(q + η, M) and then? Hard.

Maybe target q_t = (1-ρ) q + ρ flat_q (flat sum S^2). This simple. For ρ small, reduces peaks and raises lows. Or q_t = q - β (q-M)_+ and normalize. Since we project p after, scale not critical.

Which is better? Newton preserves current phase and gradually moves; direct sqrt jumps. Could do direct with damping: A_new = A * exp(0.5*(log(Q/A^2))) = A * sqrt(Q)/A? scalar Newton? Direct sqrt branch may cause discontinuity. Use log update: phase_delta = 0.5 angle(Q / A^2) wrapped; A_new = A * exp(i phase_delta) * sqrt(|Q|/|A^2|) with damping? This is like taking square root of ideal C relative to current. But Q/A is Newton unstable scalar (cycles). This is "spectral projection".

1. Helper:
   - next_pow2
   - conv_max(a, L): pad, FFT, square, inverse, return c (length 2n-1) and maybe c_max. Use real ifft.
   - normalize(a,n,S): clip 0, rescale to S (if sum small uniform).
   - project_simplex as before.
   - evaluate_score(a, L): c_max * 2n / sum^2? Actually if sum S not necessarily sqrt, compute score directly.

2. Initialization strategies:
   a) Given height_sequence_1 if exists, use and normalize to length n (if same length or interpolate? If given 8192 and we choose n same or different, can resize via interpolation/piecewise repeat/truncate). We can use as one start.
   b) Binomial decreasing: p_j = comb? Compute recursively to avoid overflow: p0=1; p_{j+1}=p_j*(2j+1)/(2j+2)? Wait (1-z)^-1/2 coefficients: p0=1, p_{j+1}=p_j*(2j+1)/(2j+2)? Core score computation:
```
def make_fft_size(n): return 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()

def convolve_self_coeffs(a, L=None):
  n=len(a); if L is None L=...
  ap=np.zeros(L); ap[:n]=a
  A=fft(ap); c=ifft(A*A).real[:2*n-1]
  return c

def score_from_coeff(a,c):
  S=a.sum(); if S<... return inf; return 2*len(a)*max(c)/S*S
```

Maybe we can use a very simple algorithm to find good sequence: Alternating square-root and projection with target max decreasing. This might converge fast and beat previous. Let's think.

Define `sr_iterate(a, n, L, target_M, max_iter, gamma)`:
```
S=1
for it:
   c=conv(a); M=max(c)
   if M <= target_M: break
   M_new = min(M*(1-red), max(target_M, M - 0.01*M?))
   q=c.copy()
   # water fill to M_new
   excess=(q-M_new).sum() positive
   if excess>0:
      # Reduce peaks to M_new; distribute to all q below M_new proportionally to (M_new-q)
      low=q<M_new
      room=(M_new - q[low]).sum()
      if room>0:
         weight = np.zeros_like(q)
         weight[low] = M_new - q[low]
         q_new = q.copy()
         q_new[q>=M_new] = M_new
         q_new[low] += excess*weight[low]/room
         q_new=np.clip(q_new,0,M_new)
      else: q_new=q
   else: q_new=q
   # normalize q_new to sum=1
   q_new /= q_new.sum()
   A=fft(pad(a)); Q=fft(pad(q_new))
   # spectral square root damped:
   C=A*A
   # compute factor exp(0.5 gamma log(Q/C))
   safeC = C + 1e-30
   ratio=Q/safeC
   # To avoid angle issues, use principal log: logabs+1j angle
   logfac = np.log(np.abs(ratio) + 1e-300) + 1j*np.angle(ratio)
   A_new = A*np.exp(0.5*gamma*logfac)
   # hermitian enforce
   A_new = 0.5*(A_new + np.conj(A_new[L[::-1]?]))
   pnew=real(ifft(A_new))[:n]
   pnew=project_simplex(pnew,1)
   # line search t in [0,1,0.5,0.25] mixing old and pnew project
   choose best score
```
This may adjust phase. However, note Q/C ratio near bins where A small can be huge; log may explode. Use clipping: amp factor = sqrt(|Q|/|C|) clipped to [0.1,10]; phase angle clipped? Since for direct sqrt, amp can be large where C zero, Q nonzero. Damping gamma and exp log can still huge. We can use target direct: A_target_mag = sqrt(|Q|); A_target_phase = 0.5 unwrap(angle(Q)) + half? Then A_new = (1-gamma) A + gamma A_target with amplitude scaling? Simpler:
   `A_target = sqrt(Q)` with phase unwrapping.
   `A_new = A + gamma*(A_target - A)` (damped).
   Enforce hermitian, ifft, project.
This avoids divide by C. The amplitude of A_target fixed by target q sum; if current q sum different? We normalized target sum=1. It should converge.
Need choose gamma small ~0.1-0.5. This is essentially enforcing A^2≈target Q. Since target Q derived from current c (flattened), this is like alternating projection between coefficient cap and square-root manifold. It can move phase significantly.
Potential issue: Taking principal sqrt of Q each iteration and damping may not increase p nonnegative; but projection keeps. It may find a good fixed point where p is as nonnegative projection of sqrt(flattened current q). Could be powerful.

Let's think about exact KKT for max cap M. Feasibility: find p_i≥0, sum1, p_i p_{k-i} sum ≤M for all k. This is quadratic. The square-root alternating projection is an intuitive way. But we can also formulate as maximum entropy distribution with additive energy constraint. Could use convex dual? Let's see if we can solve via multiplicative weights over pairs: For each coefficient k, constraint ∑_{i} p_i p_{k-i}≤M. Define potential. At optimum, active constraints. If we set p_i proportional to exp(-∑_{active k} λ_k p_{k-i}) maybe? Since derivative of constraint wrt p_i is linear p_{k-i}. This is similar to coordinate descent but linear in p (given p). Let's detail `sqrt_target_iteration`:

Function `annighet_sqrt(a, n, L, time_limit, rng, target_score, init_M_factor=0.95, warm=100)`:
Input a probability.
Set S=1.
current_best = copy, best_score.
M_desired = target_score/(2n) maybe. We can gradually approach: start M_desired = min(current_M*0.98, 1.05*target_M) maybe.
For loop until time left:
   c = conv(a); M = max(c); score=2n*M (since sum=1) (assuming normalization maintained)
   update best.
   if M <= target_M: break.
   # update M_desired: lower toward target_M
   M_desired = max(target_M, M_desired*(1.0 - eta) or target_M + 0.5*(M - target_M))
   # q target waterfill to M_desired
   q = c.copy()
   high = q > M_desired
   excess = np.sum(q[high] - M_desired)
   low = ~high
   if excess > 0:
      room = np.sum(M_desired - q[low])
      if room > 0:
         weight = np.zeros_like(q)
         weight[low] = M_desired - q[low]
         q_new = q.copy()
         q_new[high] = M_desired
         q_new[low] = q[low] + excess*weight[low]/room
         q_new = np.clip(q_new, 0, M_desired)
      else:
         q_new = np.clip(q, 0, M_desired)
   else:
      q_new = q.copy()
   # Ensure nonnegative and sum 1
   q_new = np.maximum(q_new,0)
   qsum = q_new.sum()
   if qsum < 1e-12: q_new=np.ones(2n-1);
   else q_new *= 1/qsum
   # spectral square root
   A = fft(a_pad, L) (we can reuse? compute conv already A; maybe return C and A to save)
   Q = fft(q_t_pad, L)
   # Compute A_target = sqrt(Q) with unwrapped phase
   absQ = np.abs(Q)
   phaseQ = np.unwrap(np.angle(Q))  # length L; angle at 0 maybe 0; for real q maybe phase continuous? unwrap can be expensive O(L) okay.
   A_target = np.sqrt(absQ) * np.exp(0.5j * phaseQ)
   # enforce Hermitian: Need array mapping.
   A_target = hermitian(A_target, L)
   # Damping
   gamma = adaptive (initial 0.2, maybe decay)
   A_new = (1-gamma)*A + gamma*A_target
   A_new=hermitian(A_new)
   p_raw = ifft(A_new).real[:n]
   p_new = project_simplex(p_raw,1)
   # line search: t in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1] mix between a and p_new projected (since both sum1)
   for t in [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.02]:
       cand = a + t*(p_new - a)  # sum=1, nonneg? If both nonneg convex nonneg.
       s = conv_score(cand)
       if s<best: accept
   if no improvement for many, reset M_desired closer, gamma smaller, or add noise.
```
But `hermitian` with linear frequency: Need function:
```
def make_hermitian(A):
   L=len(A)
   rev = np.empty(L)
   rev[0]=A[0]; rev[L-1]=A[L-1]
   rev[1:L-1]=A[L-1:0:-1]
   return 0.5*(A + np.conj(rev))
```

def lse_polish(a, n, L, max_time, rng, beta_temp=None):
  a=a.copy(); a=proj(a,1); y=a.copy(); t=1.0; eta=1.0; best_score=score(a); best_a=a
  stall=0; last_best=best_score; last_eta=eta
  it=0
  while time < max:
    it+=1
    c=conv(y); M=c.max(); score_y=2n*M
    if score_y<best: update
    # top_k: maybe max(16, min(2048, 2*n//32?)) adapt with score:
    if score_y > 1.6: top_k = 32? Hmm.
    # weights: sort desc, take top_k
    idx = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:top_k]
    vals = c[idx]
    # use softmax temperature = n? 
    # To avoid underflow: shift vals - vals[0]
    temp = max(1.0, n/(50*top_k))? Actually want distribute.
    # simple weights = (vals - vals[-1] + eps) / sum
    wsum = vals.sum()
    weights = vals / wsum
    # Build w_full of length L? For FFT gradient, need length L but only 2n-1 nonzero. Instead of constructing length L zeros each iter (cost), construct length 2n-1 and pad? FFT of length L needs array L. Allocate wL once maybe.
```

Let's implement projection robust:
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
  v=np.asarray(v,float)
  n=len(v)
  if n==0: return v
  w=np.sort(v)[::-1]
  cumsum=np.cumsum(w)
  idx=np.arange(1,n+1)
  theta=(cumsum-S)/idx
  rho=np.where(theta <= w)[0]
  if len(rho)==0: return np.zeros(n)??
  rho=rho.max()+1
  theta=(cumsum[rho-1]-S)/rho
  return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
This works for S>0. If v has huge negative, theta maybe. Normalize sum to S? Due numerical, can rescale.

Actually if not all v > theta, at least for last rho? There is always. Use if none rho=n.

Maybe faster use `project_simplex` from scipy? No.

```
def sqrt_annel(a, n, L, max_time, rng, target_score=1.495, verbose=False):
    a=np.clip(a,0,1); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12: a=np.ones(n)/n; else a/=s
    c,A=conv_self_A(a,L)
    cur=2*n*c.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=cur
    target_M=target_score/(2*n)
    alpha=0.1
    gamma=0.25
    stall=0
    c_pad = np.empty(L); q_pad = np.empty(L)
    while time.monotonic() < max_time:
       M=c.max()
       if M < best_score/(2*n)? Actually best_score maintained.
       # if M target reached break
       M_goal = max(target_M, (1-alpha)*target_M + alpha*M)
       if M_goal >= M*0.999: M_goal = M*0.999
       high = c > M_goal
       if high.any(): waterfill...
       else q_t = c
       # Small random perturbation to target q? maybe add noise floor.
       q_t = np.maximum(q_t,0)
       qsum=q_t.sum(); if qsum<...: ... else q_t/=qsum
       # FFT Q
       Q=fft(q_t_padded) # q_t length 2n-1 pad L
       absQ=np.abs(Q)
       phase=np.unwrap(np.angle(Q))
       A_target=np.sqrt(absQ)*exp(0.5j*phase)
       # ensure A_target[0] positive. If A_target[0]<0, A_target*=-1? This flips all (since global sign) and sum full to -1? Wait A0 = sum p_full. If A_target[0] negative, making positive by -A_target flips target? (−A)^2 same, time sequence flips; A0 positive ensures positive sum. Do if real(A_target[0])<0: A_target=-A_target.
       A_target=make_hermitian(A_target, L)
       A_new=(1-gamma)*A + gamma*A_target
       A_new=make_hermitian(A_new)
       p_raw=np.real(ifft(A_new))[:n]
       p_new=project_simplex(p_raw,1.0)
       # Maybe also p_abs?
       # evaluate p_new
       c_new, A = conv_self_A(p_new, L); cur_new=2*n*c_new.max()
       if cur_new < best_score:
           a=p_new; c=c_new; cur=cur_new; best_a=a.copy(); best_score=cur_new; stall=0; alpha=max(0, alpha*0.98)
       else:
           # try blend 0.5
           cand=a*0.5 + p_new*0.5
           c_blend,A = conv_self_A(cand,L); sc=2*n*...
           if sc < best_score:
              accept cand (sum=1)
              stall=0; alpha...
           else:
              stall+=1
              alpha=min(0.6, alpha*1.1 + 0.01)
              if stall%20==0: add noise to a? project
       A = current A for accepted (if not accepted keep old A)
    return best_a,best_score
```

Make hermitian:

def enforce_hermitian(A):

Use:
```
def make_hermitian(A):
    L = len(A)
    rev = np.empty(L, dtype=complex)
    rev[0] = A[0]
    if L > 1:
        rev[1:] = A[L-1:0:-1]
    half = A + np.conj(rev)
    half *= 0.5
    return half
```
Check for L=4: rev[1]=A[3], rev[2]=A[2], rev[3]=A[1]. Good. For m=2 (Nyquist), enforces real.

For DFT of real sequence length n zero-padded L, A[0] real. After sqrt, A[0] maybe complex with tiny imag. We can set `A_target[0]=abs(A_target[0])` (positive), `A_target[L/2]=real(A_target[L/2])` if even.

Waterfill implementation:
```
def waterfill_to_max(q, M_goal):
    q=np.maximum(q,0)
    if q.max() <= M_goal: return q
    high=q > M_goal
    excess=(q[high]-M_goal).sum()
    if excess <=0: return q
    low=~high
    room=(M_goal - q[low]).sum()
    if room <=0:
       # scale all to max M_goal? maybe set high=M_goal, low unchanged sum below
       q_new=q.copy(); q_new[high]=M_goal; return q_new
    weight = np.zeros_like(q)
    weight[low] = M_goal - q[low]
    q_new=q.copy()
    q_new[high]=M_goal
    q_new[low] += excess*weight[low]/room
    q_new=np.clip(q_new,0,M_goal)
    return q_new
```
After normalization to sum1, max may slightly exceed due scaling; we can cap again? If scaling factor >1, peaks at M_goal may exceed. To preserve max, maybe don't normalize to 1 exactly; instead target q sum should be 1. If q_new after cap has sum less than 1, scaling up violates M_goal. Alternative: choose M_goal based on q sum? The square root target sum determines p sum; if q sum not 1, p sum not 1 but we normalize p after, effectively q scaled. So we can allow q_t sum not 1; then A_target amplitude scaled; p sum will be q_t[0]? Actually DFT A0=sqrt(Q0)=sqrt(sum q_t). The full p sum = A0. After taking first n and normalizing to sum1, sequence scale changes, c scales by sum^2, score invariant. Thus target q sum can be any; normalizing p at end restores scale. But if target q sum <1, p_raw smaller and projection maybe. We can set q_t = waterfill without scaling; its max M_goal relative to sum? But target M should be relative to final sum1. If q sum not 1, max allowed for sum1 is M_goal*(1/qsum)^2? Wait if q_t corresponds to unnormalized p0 with sum S_t = sqrt(Q0)? Let's avoid complexity: Keep q_t sum 1. To keep max ≤M_goal after scaling, if waterfill sum <1, scaling up raises max. We can set M_goal slightly lower or after scaling cap. But ideally waterfill with cap and redistribution should preserve sum: We set high to M_goal, add excess to lows using full room (M_goal - q_low). The total room is exactly enough to absorb excess while never exceeding M_goal. Thus sum after operation should remain original sum (1). Let's verify: high sum decreases by excess. Lows increase total by excess (since weight sum room, add excess*weight/room -> total excess). Caps not binding except high; lows at most M_goal. So sum unchanged exactly if room≥0. Good. Thus qsum=1. But due floating, okay. So normalization hardly needed. If room<0 (all at M_goal), infeasible; then sum preserved by capping? sum decreases. But if all at M_goal and original sum1, M_goal*length=1, okay. If not, qsum maybe 1. So we can normalize only tiny drift. After q_new, if qsum>0, q_new /= qsum. If scaling factor 1+eps, max may exceed by eps; project p and next iteration handles.

def lse_polish(a, n, L, max_time, rng, target_score=1.49, beta_schedule=None):
   a=proj(a,1); best_a=a; best_score=2n*max_conv; y=a.copy(); t=1; eta=1.0; eta_min=0.01; stall=0
   wL=np.zeros(L)
   top_init=max(16, n//256?) ; top_max=min(4096, 2*n?)
   while time...
      if time.time for unwind..
      c,A=conv_self_A(y,L)
      cur=2n*max(c)
      if cur<best: update
      if cur <= target_score/(??) break? target_score.
      top_k = min(top_max, top_init + it//20)
      idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:top_k]
      vals=c[idx]
      # maybe weights = vals / vals.sum()
      # For softmax: w=np.exp((vals-vals.max())*temp); but vals mostly similar.
      weights=vals/vals.sum()
      wL.fill(0); wL[0:2*n-1]? We need assign idx. Since wL zero each iter cost. Use wL[:]=0; wL[idx]=weights
      W=fft(wL)
      grad=2*np.real(ifft(W*np.conj(A)))[:n]
      gnorm=np.linalg.norm(grad)
      if gnorm<1e-14: break
      # direction
      # try eta values [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25, eta*2]
      accepted=False
      for f in [1.0,0.5,0.25,2.0]:
          eta_t=eta*f
          x=project_simplex(y - eta_t*grad,1.0)
          # quick score every maybe: c_x, _=conv_self_A(x,L); sc=2n*max
          if sc <= max(cur, best_score)*(1.0+1e-12): accept
      if not accept: x=y; sc=cur; fail
      # Nesterov update
      t_old=t
      t=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t))
      y_next=x + ((t_old-1)/t)*(x - a)
      y=project_simplex(y_next,1.0)
      a=x
      # line search eta if improved else adjust
      if sc < best_score: stall=0; eta=min(10, eta*1.05)
      else:
        stall+=1
        if stall>50: eta=max(0.1, eta*0.7)
        if stall>150: y=best_a.copy(); a=y.copy(); t=1; add noise
```

    q = np.empty(2*n-1)
    if q_mode=='flat': q.fill(1.0/(2*n-1))
    else: q from triangular? maybe linear ramp? 
    Q=fft(q padded)
    absQ=np.abs(Q); phase=np.angle(Q); phase[absQ<1e-12]=0; phase=np.unwrap(phase)

    p=project_simplex(p_full,1)
    # try abs version

    # choose better score

def init_binomial(n, reverse=False, shift=0.0):
    w=np.empty(n)
    w[0]=1.0
    for j in range(1,n): w[j]=w[j-1]*(2*j-1)/(2*j)
    # apply shift: w *= (1+shift*np.linspace(0,1,n))? maybe not
    if reverse: w=w[::-1]
    return normalize_prob(w)

def resize_seq(x, m):
   x=np.clip(x,0,1000)
   if len(x)==0: return np.ones(m)/m
   if len(x)==m: x=x.copy()
   elif len(x)>m: indices=np.linspace(0,len(x)-1,m); x=np.interp(indices, np.arange(len(x)), x)
   else: out=np.zeros(m); seg=m/len(x); for i... use linear interpolation/nearest
   normalize_prob
```

def frequency_square_root_step(A, Q, gamma):
    C = A*A
    absC = np.abs(C)
    ratio = Q / (C + 1e-30)
    abs_ratio = np.abs(ratio)
    logabs = np.log(abs_ratio)
    logabs = np.clip(logabs, -2.0, 2.0)
    ang = np.angle(ratio)
    # To keep Hermitian, maybe not clip angle. Use step=0.5*gamma
    step = 0.5*gamma
    update = np.exp(step*(logabs + 1j*ang))
    A_new = A * update
    A_new = make_hermitian(A_new)
    # Normalize A[0]? The sum constraint: after hermitian, A[0] may have small imag. Set A_new[0]=real(A_new[0])? But if amplitude changed at DC, sum changes. We want target q sum maybe 1; current A0=1; Q0~1; log0=0, so A0~1. To enforce sum1, set A_new[0]=1.0? But if target qsum !=1 due drift, maybe. We can set A_new[0]=real(A_new[0]) and real(A_new[L/2]) if even. Then projection normalizes sum anyway. For gradient, maybe preserve target sum: set A_new[0]=sqrt(max(Q[0],0))? Q0 is float? Q[0]=sum q_t ~1. Set `A_new[0]=np.sqrt(max(float(np.real(Q[0])),0))`. This allows sum adjust. But A[0] real positive. Do. If L even, set [L/2]=real.
```
This step is continuous in phase and amplitude. It may take many small changes; gamma can be 0.5. It directly enforces A^2≈Q without branch jumps.

Let's detail `frequency_square_root_step`:
```
def frequency_square_root_step(A, Q, gamma):
    C = A * A
    # Add small regularizer to avoid zero
    reg = 1e-30
    ratio = Q / (C + reg)
    abs_ratio = np.abs(ratio)
    # Clip log to avoid huge amplitude changes
    logabs = np.log(abs_ratio + 1e-300)
    # If ratio is zero (Q zero, C nonzero), logabs large negative; clip -1.5 maybe
    logabs = np.clip(logabs, -1.5, 1.5)
    ang = np.angle(ratio)
    # For zero Q, angle arbitrary; set update amplitude to exp(0.5gamma*logabs) ~0.5 if ratio 0? But if abs_ratio underflow to 0, logabs -large, clip to -1.5, not zero. Could force update 0 where absQ < eps? 
    epsQ = 1e-14
    zeroQ = np.abs(Q) < epsQ * max(1.0, np.max(np.abs(Q)))
    if np.any(zeroQ): logabs[zeroQ] = -5.0  # reduce strongly? But if current C nonzero and target Q zero, we want A ->0. Could be too aggressive. Use -4.
    step = 0.5*gamma
    A_new = A * np.exp(step * (logabs + 1j*ang))
    A_new = make_hermitian(A_new)
    # DC: set to sqrt(Q0 real) positive
    if Q[0].real > 0:
       A_new[0]=np.sqrt(Q[0].real)
    else: A_new[0]=0.0
    if L % 2 == 0: A_new[L//2] = np.real(A_new[L//2])
    return A_new
```
But if Q0 slightly complex due numerical, use real. For q nonnegative, Q0 real.

Clipping logabs -1.5 to 1.5 limits amplitude ratio per step to e^0.75=2.11 (step 0.5 gamma=1?). Actually step=0.5*gamma; logabs clipped ±2, step 0.5, exp ±1=2.7. Could set clip ±3. Good. For zero Q, logabs set -4, step* -4 = -2 -> A reduced by 0.135, not zero. Maybe many steps. Fine.

Let's implement `project_simplex` carefully for speed:
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum = np.cumsum(w)
    idx = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    theta = (cumsum - S) / idx
    # valid where theta <= w
    # We need largest rho such that exists? Standard: rho = max {j | theta_j <= w_j}; theta nondecreasing? w sorted descending, cumsum-S over j. There is known.
    valid = theta <= w
    if not np.any(valid):
        # should not happen if sum w >= S; if all v negative and S>0, project zero? But sum zero + theta? If sum w < S, no valid; return zeros? Need maintain sum S impossible with nonnegative if S>0? Actually with nonnegative and no upper, any S possible by shifting negative? Projection to simplex always exists; formula valid should find. If all v very negative, theta maybe? Example v=[-1,-1], S=1: w[-1,-1], cumsum[-1,-2], theta[-2,-1], valid theta<=w? -2<=-1 true at j=1? tau. Should find.
        rho = n
    else:
        rho = int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0]) + 1)
    theta = (cumsum[rho - 1] - S) / rho
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    out *= S / out.sum() if sum>0 else 0
    return out
```
The rescale ensures sum exactly S but may reintroduce if sum zero? If out sum zero impossible unless S=0; but clip. If sum zero, assign uniform? But S>0. Due overflow? okay.

Potential performance: `np.where(valid)` allocates. Could use `np.nonzero(valid)[0][-1]`. Fine. Need if valid none: maybe set out uniform? Use fallback.

def normalize_prob(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   a=np.clip(a,0,1) # or 1000? But for init, values O1; clip 0,1000
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-12: return np.full(len(a),1.0/len(a))
   return a/s
```

def make_inits(n, L, rng):
  inits=[]
  g=get_given(); if g is not None: inits.append(('given', resize_sequence(g,n)))
  # binomial
  b=init_binomial(n,False); inits.append(('bindec',b))
  binrev=init_binomial(n,True); inits.append(('bininc',binrev))
  # mix bindec and reverse
  mix=0.8*b+0.2*binrev; inits.append(('mix',normalize_prob(mix)))
  # smooth random: base b? maybe b*(1+0.3 sin random phase), normalize/clip

  r=fb*(1+0.25*np.sin(2*np.pi*(1.3*np.arange(n)+rng.uniform(0,1))/n + 0.1*rng.normal(0,1,n)); clip positive
  inits.append(('smooth',normalize_prob(r)))
  # flat sqrt direct maybe

  except: pass
  return inits

Direct flat init using principal sqrt and phase unwrap:
```
def init_flat_direct(n,L):
  q=np.ones(2*n-1)
  q/=q.sum()
  qpad=np.zeros(L); qpad[:2*n-1]=q
  Q=fft(qpad)
  absQ=np.abs(Q)
  phase=np.angle(Q); phase[absQ<1e-12]=0; phase=np.unwrap(phase)
  A=np.sqrt(absQ)*np.exp(0.5j*phase)
  if A[0].real<0: A=-A
  A=make_hermitian(A)
  p_full=np.real(ifft(A))[:n]
  # Try p_full and abs(p_full), and maybe negative lobes? Choose after projection/lse quick? Just project both and return best score.
  p1=project_simplex(p_full,1)
  p2=project_simplex(np.abs(p_full),1)
  c1,_ = conv_self_A(p1,L); s1=2*n*max(c1)
  c2,_ = conv_self_A(p2,L); s2=2*n*max(c2)
  return p1 if s1<s2 else p2
```
But p_full sum maybe 1; if p_full has negative, projection. Could be okay.

Now, `improve_loop` or `refine_sequence` that alternates sqrt and LSE until time:
```
def refine_sequence(a, n, L, budget, rng):
  t_end=time.monotonic()+budget
  a=normalize...
  c,A=conv; cur=score
  best_a=a; best_score=cur
  current_target = 1.49
  cycles=0
  while time.monotonic() < t_end - 5:
     target = max(1.43, min(best_score*0.998, 1.49) if best_score<inf else 1.49)
     # alternate sqrt and lse chunk
     chunk = min(60.0, (t_end - time.monotonic())*0.25)
     a1,s1=sqrt_annel(a,n,L,chunk,rng,target)
     if s1 < best_score: best_score=s1; best_a=a1.copy(); a=a1.copy()
     else: a=a1.copy()? Maybe if no improve, keep a1 small perturb.
     if time left:
        a2,s2=lse_polish(a,n,L,chunk*0.7,rng,target)
        if s2<best: update
     cycles+=1
     if best unchanged for cycles>6: add noise to a from best

Pseudo full:
```
def sqrt_annel(a, n, L, max_time, rng, target_score):
    a=normalize_prob(a.copy())
    c,A=conv_self_A(a,L)
    cur=2.0*n*c.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=cur
    target_M=target_score/(2.0*n)
    if target_M <=0: target_M=cur/(2*n)*0.99
    alpha=0.1
    gamma=0.35
    stall=0
    t0=time.monotonic(); t_end=t0+max_time
    noise_every=0
    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
        M=c.max()
        if cur < best_score: update (should)
        if cur <= target_score + 1e-9: break
        # M_goal
        M_goal = max(target_M, (1.0-alpha)*target_M + alpha*M)
        if M_goal >= M:
            M_goal = M*0.999
        # waterfill
        q = waterfill_to_max(c, M_goal)
        q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
        qsum = q.sum()
        if qsum < 1e-12: q=np.ones(2*n-1)/(2*n-1)
        else: q = q / qsum
        qpad=np.zeros(L); qpad[:2*n-1]=q
        Q=fft(qpad)
        A_new=freq_square_root_step(A, Q, gamma)
        p_new=project_simplex(np.real(ifft(A_new))[:n], 1.0)
        # maybe p_abs variant? Could be costly. Try if p_new not improve.
        best_local_score=cur; best_local_a=a; best_local_c=c; best_local_A=A
        for tval in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1):
             cand = a + tval*(p_new-a) # nonnegative sum1
             c_t, A_t = conv_self_A(cand, L)
             sc = 2*n*c_t.max()
             if sc < best_local_score - 1e-14:
                 best_local_score=sc; best_local_a=cand.copy(); best_local_c=c_t; best_local_A=A_t
                 if sc <= target_score: break
        if best_local_score < best_score:
             a=best_local_a; c=best_local_c; A=best_local_A; cur=best_local_score
             best_a=a.copy(); best_score=cur; stall=0
             if alpha > 0.02: alpha = max(0.02, alpha*0.97)
             else: alpha=0.02
             if gamma < 0.6: gamma = min(0.6, gamma*1.05)
        else:
             stall += 1
             # keep a unchanged, but maybe move a little toward p_new if very close? no.
             if stall == 20: gamma = max(0.1, gamma*0.7)
             if stall == 40: alpha = min(0.5, alpha*1.2 + 0.01)
             if stall == 80:
                 # noise
                 scale = 0.005 / np.sqrt(n)
                 a_noisy = project_simplex(a + rng.normal(0.0, scale, n), 1.0)
                 c_n, A_n=conv_self_A(a_noisy, L)
                 sc_n=2*n*c_n.max()
                 if sc_n < max(best_score*1.01, cur):
                     a=a_noisy; c=c_n; A=A_n; cur=sc_n
                 else:
                     # revert to best for exploration? Use best_a plus noise
                     a = project_simplex(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, scale*2, n), 1.0)
                     c,A=conv_self_A(a,L); cur=2*n*c.max()
                 stall=0; gamma=0.3
    return best_a, best_score
```

def refine_sequence(a, n, L, budget, rng):
  t_end=time.monotonic()+max(0,budget)
  a=normalize_prob(a)
  c,A=conv; cur=score; best_a=a; best_score=cur
  # A sqrt big chunk
  target = choose_target(best_score)
  sqrt_budget = min(budget*0.55, t_end - time)
  a1,s1=sqrt_annel(a,n,L,sqrt_budget,rng,target)
  if s1<best: update
  # LSE chunk
  rem=...
  target=choose_target(best)
  lse_budget=min(0.25*budget, rem*0.4)
  a2,s2=lse_polish(a,n,L,lse_budget,rng,target)
  if s2<best: update
  # remaining alternating
  cycles=0
  while time < t_end-5:
      remaining=t_end-time
      chunk=min(60.0, remaining*0.3)
      target=choose_target(best)
      if cycles%2==0:
           a1,s1=sqrt_annel(a,n,L,chunk,rng,target)
      else:
           a2,s2=lse_polish(a,n,L,chunk,rng,target)
      if s<best: update; a=b a
      else:
           # if no improve and cycles>3, jitter a from best
      cycles++
  return best
```

Let's detail `freq_square_root_step` zero Q handling:
```
def frequency_square_root_step(A, Q, gamma):
    C = A*A
    absC = np.abs(C)
    # Regularize where C small
    eps = 1e-30
    ratio = Q / (C + eps)
    abs_ratio = np.abs(ratio)
    logabs = np.log(abs_ratio + 1e-300)
    # Limit amplitude changes
    logabs = np.clip(logabs, -3.0, 3.0)
    # Where target is nearly zero, reduce more strongly but not to zero in one shot
    qscale = np.max(np.abs(Q))
    zero_target = np.abs(Q) < 1e-12 * max(1.0, qscale)
    if np.any(zero_target):
        logabs[zero_target] = -4.0
    ang = np.angle(ratio)
    step = 0.5 * gamma
    # For bins where target is exactly zero, set angle zero to avoid noise
    if np.any(zero_target): ang[zero_target]=0.0
    A_new = A * np.exp(step * (logabs + 1j * ang))
    A_new = make_hermitian(A_new)
    # Fix DC and Nyquist
    q0 = float(np.real(Q[0]))
    if q0 > 0.0:
        A_new[0] = np.sqrt(q0)
    else:
        A_new[0] = 0.0
    if len(A_new) % 2 == 0:
        A_new[len(A_new)//2] = float(np.real(A_new[len(A_new)//2]))
    return A_new
```
If `A` has zero at some bins and Q nonzero, ratio huge, logabs clip to 3, step<=0.5 -> exp <=e^1.5=4.5, okay. If gamma=0.7 step .35 -> exp exp1.05? fine.

Should we preserve A[0] if target qsum not 1? We set A0=sqrt(q0). If current A0=1, q0 after waterfill sum maybe 1; okay. If projection changes p sum to1, next A0=1. Good.

`waterfill_to_max`:
```
def waterfill_to_max(q, M):
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    if M <= 0: return q
    if q.max() <= M: return q
    high = q > M
    excess = float(np.sum(q[high] - M))
    if excess <= 0: return q
    low = ~high
    room = float(np.sum(M - q[low]))
    q_new = q.copy()
    q_new[high] = M
    if room > 1e-15:
        weight = np.zeros_like(q)
        weight[low] = M - q[low]
        q_new[low] += excess * weight[low] / room
        q_new[np.where(q_new > M + 1e-12)[0]] = M  # no assign where?
        q_new = np.minimum(q_new, M)
        q_new = np.maximum(q_new, 0.0)
    # if room not enough, sum decreased; okay
    return q_new
```
Using `np.where` in assignment okay no lambda. To avoid modifying view: `q_new[high]=M` then lows. If q_new[low] exceed due numerical, clip. But if all high and low empty room=0, qsum less than original. Then normalize will scale, max >M. But if all high target infeasible. Rare. Could if target M below average? We choose above average. If target infeasible average may >M? If M< avg, room negative? Actually if all q >M, room=0, qsum=(2n-1)M <1 if M<avg, scaling makes max >M. Fine.

def lse_polish(a, n, L, max_time, rng, target_score):
   a=normalize_prob(a.copy())
   c,A=conv_self_A(a,L); cur=2n*max; best_a=a.copy(); best_score=cur
   y=a.copy(); prev_x=a.copy(); t=1.0; eta=2.0; stall=0; it=0
   wL=np.zeros(L)
   t_end=...
   while time < t_end:
       it+=1; c,A=conv_self_A(y,L); sc_y=...
       if sc_y<best: update
       if best_score <= target_score+1e-9: break
       top_k=...
       if top_k >= len(c): idx=np.arange(len(c)); vals=c
       else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]; vals=c[idx]
       weights=vals/vals.sum()
       wL.fill(0); wL[idx]=weights
       W=fft(wL); grad=2*np.real(ifft(W*np.conj(A)))[:n]; gnorm=norm
       if gnorm<1e-14: break
       x=project_simplex(y - eta*grad, 1.0)
       c_x, A_x=conv_self_A(x,L); sc_x=...
       threshold=max(sc_y,best_score)
       if sc_x > threshold*1.05:
          eta=max(0.1, eta*0.6)
          x=project_simplex(y - eta*grad, 1.0); c_x,A_x=conv_self_A(x,L); sc_x=...
       if sc_x < best_score:
           update best; stall=0; eta=min(8, eta*1.05)
       else:
           stall+=1
           if stall > 50: eta=max(0.2, eta*0.85)
           if stall > 120:
               t=1.0; prev_x=best_a.copy(); y=best_a.copy();
               noise=0.002/np.sqrt(n); y=project_simplex(y+rng.normal(0,noise,n),1); stall=0; eta=2
       # momentum update
       t_old=t
       t=0.5*(1+np.sqrt(1+4*t*t))
       mu=(t_old-1)/t
       y_next=x + mu*(x-prev_x)
       y=project_simplex(y_next,1.0)
       prev_x=x
   return best_a,best_score
```

Now, let's think about mathematical correctness of frequency step: C=A*A (not A*conj(A)). Since A is DFT of real p, A^2 corresponds to cyclic convolution p*p length L if L≥2n-1 and no wrap? Yes, DFT theorem: DFT(a_pad)*DFT(a_pad) = DFT(cyclic_conv(a_pad,a_pad)). Since length L≥2n-1, cyclic conv equals linear conv zero-padded (no wrap). Good. So C exactly Q_current. Target Q. Factor moves A^2 toward Q.

When we take first n of ifft(A_new), we discard tail; the linear conv of p_new is not exactly from A_new^2 but next iteration recomputes. Good.

Let's double-check `make_hermitian` for L=1: rev arrays. L for n≥4? n maybe 1? Not. But handle:
```
def make_hermitian(A):
    L=len(A); rev=np.empty(L, dtype=np.complex128); rev[0]=A[0]
    if L>1: rev[1:]=A[L-1:0:-1]
    return 0.5*(A+np.conj(rev))
```

`project_simplex` sum fix:
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum = np.cumsum(w)
    idx = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    theta = (cumsum - S) / idx
    valid = theta <= w
    if np.any(valid):
        rho = int(np.nonzero(valid)[0][-1] + 1)
    else:
        rho = n
    theta = (cumsum[rho - 1] - S) / rho
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = out.sum()
    if s > 1e-15:
        out *= (S / s)
    else:
        out = np.full(n, S / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return out
```
If S=0? We call S=1. In fallback maybe. Good.

```python
"""
Hybrid search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a)**2).

Main ideas:
- Work on the probability simplex (sum(a)=1).  The objective becomes 2*n*max_c.
- Use FFT-based linear convolution for all internal evaluations.
- Primary novelty: alternating "convolution square-root" spectral steps.
  Given a current coefficient vector c = a*a, form a flattened target q
  (water-filled to a desired peak cap, sometimes with power-smoothing).
  In the DFT domain, move A = FFT(a) toward a square root of Q = FFT(q)
  by damped complex-log updates A <- A * exp(0.5*gamma*log(Q/A^2)),
  then project the truncated inverse transform back to the simplex.
- Secondary polish: Nesterov-style projected subgradient steps on top-lag
  surrogates of max(c).
- Multi-start with analytic binomial/power-law roots, reference sequence,
  flat square-root heuristic, and smooth perturbations.
- Sequential two-length search (8192, then 16384) with nesting/upsampling.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.fft import fft, ifft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import fft, ifft


def next_pow2(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_prob(a):
    """Clip to [0, 1000] and rescale to sum exactly 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (1.0 / s)


def project_simplex(v, S):
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=S}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum = np.cumsum(w)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    theta = (cumsum - S) / idx
    valid = theta <= w
    if np.any(valid):
        rho = int(np.nonzero(valid)[0][-1] + 1)
    else:
        rho = n
    theta = (cumsum[rho - 1] - S) / rho
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s > 1e-15:
        out *= (S / s)
    else:
        out = np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return out


def make_hermitian(A):
    """Enforce Hermitian symmetry of a DFT array."""
    L = A.size
    if L == 1:
        return np.array([np.real(A[0])], dtype=np.complex128)
    rev = np.empty(L, dtype=np.complex128)
    rev[0] = A[0]
    rev[1:] = A[:0:-1]
    return 0.5 * (A + np.conj(rev))


def conv_self_A(a, L):
    """Linear self-convolution and FFT of a, padded to length L."""
    n = a.size
    ap = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    ap[:n] = a
    A = fft(ap)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A))[:2 * n - 1]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c, A


def score_from_c(c, n):
    """Objective value for probability-scale sequence from convolution c."""
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def eval_prob(a, L):
    """Return (c, A, score) for probability-scale a."""
    c, A = conv_self_A(a, L)
    return c, A, score_from_c(c, a.size)


def waterfill_to_max(q, M):
    """
    Water-fill projection of a nonnegative q onto max <= M, preserving sum
    as well as possible.  Peak excess is redistributed to lags below M
    proportionally to their remaining headroom.
    """
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    if M <= 0.0:
        return q
    if q.size == 0:
        return q
    if float(np.max(q)) <= M:
        return q.copy()

    high = q > M
    excess = float(np.sum(q[high] - M))
    if excess <= 1e-18:
        return q.copy()

    q_new = q.copy()
    q_new[high] = M
    low = ~high
    if np.any(low):
        headroom = M - q_new[low]
        sroom = float(headroom.sum())
        if sroom > 1e-18:
            q_new[low] += excess * headroom / sroom
    q_new = np.clip(q_new, 0.0, max(M, 1e-300))
    return q_new


def frequency_square_root_step(A, Q, gamma):
    """
    Damped spectral move toward A_new^2 ~= Q.

    Uses A_new = A * exp(0.5*gamma*log(Q/A^2)), which preserves the current
    spectral branch and avoids abrupt principal-square-root phase jumps.
    """
    A = make_hermitian(A)
    C = A * A
    eps = 1e-30
    ratio = Q / (C + eps)
    abs_ratio = np.abs(ratio)
    logabs = np.log(abs_ratio + 1e-300)
    logabs = np.clip(logabs, -3.0, 3.0)

    qscale = float(np.max(np.abs(Q))) if Q.size else 0.0
    zero_target = np.abs(Q) < 1e-12 * max(1.0, qscale)
    ang = np.angle(ratio)
    if np.any(zero_target):
        logabs[zero_target] = -4.0
        ang[zero_target] = 0.0

    step = 0.5 * gamma
    A_new = A * np.exp(step * (logabs + 1j * ang))
    A_new = make_hermitian(A_new)

    q0 = float(np.real(Q[0]))
    if q0 > 0.0:
        A_new[0] = np.sqrt(q0)
    else:
        A_new[0] = 0.0
    if A_new.size % 2 == 0:
        mid = A_new.size // 2
        A_new[mid] = float(np.real(A_new[mid]))
    return A_new


def sqrt_annel(a, n, L, max_time, rng, target_score):
    """
    Alternating square-root / water-filled target flattening.
    Returns best probability-scale sequence and its internal score.
    """
    a = normalize_prob(a.copy().astype(np.float64))
    c, A = conv_self_A(a, L)
    A = make_hermitian(A)
    cur = score_from_c(c, n)
    if not np.isfinite(cur):
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n)
        c, A = conv_self_A(a, L)
        cur = score_from_c(c, n)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = cur

    target_M = target_score / (2.0 * n)
    if target_M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(target_M):
        target_M = cur / (2.0 * n) * 0.99

    alpha = 0.10
    gamma = 0.35
    stall = 0
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    t_end = t0 + max(0.0, float(max_time))
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
        it += 1
        if best_score <= target_score + 1e-12:
            break

        M = float(np.max(c))
        M_goal = max(target_M, (1.0 - alpha) * target_M + alpha * M)
        if M_goal >= M:
            M_goal = max(target_M, M * 0.999)

        # Occasionally use a power-smoothed target in addition to raw waterfill.
        if it % 7 == 3:
            q = np.power(np.maximum(c, 1e-15), 0.70)
            qs = float(q.sum())
            if qs <= 1e-15:
                q = np.ones(2 * n - 1) / (2.0 * n - 1.0)
            else:
                q /= qs
            q = waterfill_to_max(q, M_goal)
        else:
            q = waterfill_to_max(c, M_goal)

        qs = float(q.sum())
        if qs <= 1e-15:
            q = np.ones(2 * n - 1) / (2.0 * n - 1.0)
        else:
            q = q / qs

        qpad = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        qpad[:2 * n - 1] = q
        Q = fft(qpad)

        # Finer gamma when close to target.
        gamma_used = 0.15 if M < target_M * 1.05 else gamma
        A_new = frequency_square_root_step(A, Q, gamma_used)
        p_new = project_simplex(np.real(ifft(A_new))[:n], 1.0)

        # Line search on the convex mixture between current and candidate.
        local_best_score = cur
        local_best_a = a
        local_best_c = c
        local_best_A = A
        accepted = False

        for tv in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
            cand = a + tv * (p_new - a)
            cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
            cs = float(cand.sum())
            if cs <= 1e-15:
                cand = p_new
            else:
                cand = cand / cs
            c_t, A_t = conv_self_A(cand, L)
            sc_t = score_from_c(c_t, n)
            if np.isfinite(sc_t) and sc_t < local_best_score - 1e-15:
                local_best_score = sc_t
                local_best_a = cand
                local_best_c = c_t
                local_best_A = A_t
                accepted = True
                if sc_t <= target_score:
                    break

        if accepted:
            a = local_best_a
            c = local_best_c
            A = make_hermitian(local_best_A)
            cur = local_best_score
            if cur < best_score:
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_score = cur
            stall = 0
            if alpha > 0.02:
                alpha = max(0.02, alpha * 0.97)
            if gamma < 0.55:
                gamma = min(0.55, gamma * 1.02)
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall == 20:
                gamma = max(0.10, gamma * 0.70)
            if stall == 40:
                alpha = min(0.50, alpha * 1.20 + 0.01)
            if stall == 80:
                scale = 0.005 / np.sqrt(float(n))
                noisy = project_simplex(a + rng.normal(0.0, scale, n), 1.0)
                c_n, A_n = conv_self_A(noisy, L)
                sc_n = score_from_c(c_n, n)
                if np.isfinite(sc_n) and sc_n < max(best_score * 1.02, cur):
                    a = noisy
                    c = c_n
                    A = make_hermitian(A_n)
                    cur = sc_n
                else:
                    noisy = project_simplex(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, 2.0 * scale, n), 1.0)
                    c, A = conv_self_A(noisy, L)
                    A = make_hermitian(A)
                    a = noisy
                    cur = score_from_c(c, n)
                stall = 0
                gamma = 0.30

    return best_a, best_score


def lse_polish(a, n, L, max_time, rng, target_score):
    """
    Nesterov-style projected subgradient polish using top-lag weights.
    Returns best probability-scale sequence and its internal score.
    """
    a = normalize_prob(a.copy().astype(np.float64))
    c, A = conv_self_A(a, L)
    cur = score_from_c(c, n)
    if not np.isfinite(cur):
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n)
        c, A = conv_self_A(a, L)
        cur = score_from_c(c, n)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = cur

    y = a.copy()
    prev_x = a.copy()
    t = 1.0
    eta = 2.0
    stall = 0
    it = 0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    t_end = t0 + max(0.0, float(max_time))

    wL = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)

    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
        it += 1
        if best_score <= target_score + 1e-12:
            break

        c_y, A_y = conv_self_A(y, L)
        sc_y = score_from_c(c_y, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc_y) and sc_y < best_score:
            best_score = sc_y
            best_a = y.copy()

        m = c_y.size
        top_init = 32
        top_max = min(4096, max(256, n // 8))
        top_k = min(top_max, top_init + it // 18)

        if top_k >= m:
            idx = np.arange(m)
            vals = c_y.copy()
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c_y, -top_k)[-top_k:]
            vals = c_y[idx]

        wsum = float(vals.sum())
        if wsum <= 1e-18:
            weights = np.zeros(top_k)
            if top_k > 0:
                weights[0] = 1.0
        else:
            weights = vals / wsum

        wL.fill(0.0)
        wL[idx] = weights
        W = fft(wL)
        grad = 2.0 * np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A_y)))[:n]
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            break

        x = project_simplex(y - eta * grad, 1.0)
        c_x, _ = conv_self_A(x, L)
        sc_x = score_from_c(c_x, n)
        threshold = max(sc_y, best_score)
        if np.isfinite(sc_x) and sc_x > threshold * 1.05:
            eta = max(0.1, eta * 0.6)
            x = project_simplex(y - eta * grad, 1.0)
            c_x, _ = conv_self_A(x, L)
            sc_x = score_from_c(c_x, n)

        if not np.isfinite(sc_x) or sc_x > max(threshold * 3.0, 1.0):
            x = best_a.copy()
            sc_x = best_score
            eta = max(0.1, eta * 0.5)
            stall += 20

        if np.isfinite(sc_x) and sc_x < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = sc_x
            x = x.copy()
            best_a = x.copy()
            stall = 0
            eta = min(8.0, eta * 1.03)
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 60:
                eta = max(0.2, eta * 0.88)

        if stall > 140:
            t = 1.0
            prev_x = best_a.copy()
            scale = 0.002 / np.sqrt(float(n))
            y = project_simplex(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, scale, n), 1.0)
            stall = 0
            eta = 2.0

        t_old = t
        t = 0.5 * (1.0 + np.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t * t))
        mu = (t_old - 1.0) / t
        y_next = x + mu * (x - prev_x)
        y = project_simplex(y_next, 1.0)
        prev_x = x

    return best_a, best_score


def init_binomial(n, reverse=False):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2) truncated to length n.
    This is the analytic infinite square root of the flat one-sided sequence.
    """
    w = np.empty(max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return w
    w[0] = 1.0
    for j in range(1, n):
        w[j] = w[j - 1] * (2 * j - 1) / (2 * j)
    if reverse:
        w = w[::-1]
    return normalize_prob(w)


def resize_sequence(x, m):
    """Resize a sequence to length m by linear interpolation and normalize."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.full(m, 1.0 / m)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if x.size == 1:
        out = np.full(m, x[0])
    else:
        xp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, x.size)
        yp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
        out = np.interp(yp, xp, x)
        out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return normalize_prob(out)


def get_reference_sequence():
    """Return height_sequence_1 if available and valid."""
    try:
        val = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(val, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return arr


def init_flat_direct(n, L):
    """
    Direct spectral square-root of an exactly flat target q.
    Used only as an initialization; the main refinement uses damped updates.
    """
    m = 2 * n - 1
    q = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    q /= m
    qpad = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    qpad[:m] = q
    Q = fft(qpad)

    absQ = np.abs(Q)
    phase = np.angle(Q)
    small = absQ < 1e-12 * max(1.0, float(np.max(absQ)))
    phase[small] = 0.0
    phase = np.unwrap(phase)

    A = np.sqrt(absQ) * np.exp(0.5j * phase)
    if np.real(A[0]) < 0.0:
        A = -A
    A = make_hermitian(A)
    p_full = np.real(ifft(A))[:n]

    p1 = project_simplex(p_full, 1.0)
    p2 = project_simplex(np.abs(p_full), 1.0)

    c1, _ = conv_self_A(p1, L)
    c2, _ = conv_self_A(p2, L)
    s1 = score_from_c(c1, n)
    s2 = score_from_c(c2, n)
    p_best = p1 if s1 < s2 else p2
    s_best = min(s1, s2)
    if not np.isfinite(s_best):
        return normalize_prob(np.ones(n) / n)
    return p_best


def make_inits(n, L, rng, seed_seq=None):
    """Generate diverse probability-scale initializations."""
    inits = []

    if seed_seq is not None and np.asarray(seed_seq).size > 0:
        inits.append(("seed", resize_sequence(seed_seq, n)))

    ref = get_reference_sequence()
    if ref is not None:
        inits.append(("ref", resize_sequence(ref, n)))

    b = init_binomial(n, False)
    br = init_binomial(n, True)
    inits.append(("binom", b))
    inits.append(("rev", br))
    inits.append(("mix", normalize_prob(0.80 * b + 0.20 * br)))

    # Power-law with different exponent and shift.
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    pw = np.power(idx + 2.0, -0.45)
    inits.append(("power", normalize_prob(pw)))

    # Smooth wave-modulated binomial.
    tvec = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    f0 = 1.0 + 2.0 * float(rng.random())
    p0 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
    p1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
    wave = 1.0
    wave += 0.30 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * (2.0 * f0) * tvec + p0)
    wave += 0.12 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * (7.0 * f0) * tvec + p1)
    wave += 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(n)
    wv = np.clip(b * wave, 1e-9, 1000.0)
    inits.append(("wave", normalize_prob(wv)))

    if L is not None:
        try:
            pflat = init_flat_direct(n, L)
            if pflat is not None and pflat.size == n:
                inits.append(("flat", pflat))
        except Exception:
            pass

    if len(inits) == 0:
        inits.append(("uniform", np.ones(n) / n))
    return inits


def choose_target(current_score):
    """Pick an optimization target slightly below current score."""
    if not np.isfinite(current_score):
        return 1.49
    target = current_score * 0.995
    if target > 1.49:
        target = 1.49
    if target < 1.42:
        target = 1.42
    return float(target)


def refine_sequence(a, n, L, budget, rng):
    """Sequential/alternating refinement using square-root and LSE polish."""
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget))
    if budget <= 1.0:
        c, _, s = eval_prob(normalize_prob(a), L)
        return a.copy(), s

    a = normalize_prob(a.copy())
    c, _, cur = eval_prob(a, L)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = cur

    t_end = time.monotonic() + budget

    # Large square-root phase.
    sqrt_budget = min(0.55 * budget, max(0.0, t_end - time.monotonic()))
    if sqrt_budget > 10.0:
        target = choose_target(best_score)
        a1, s1 = sqrt_annel(a, n, L, sqrt_budget, rng, target)
        if np.isfinite(s1) and s1 < best_score:
            best_score = float(s1)
            best_a = a1

    # LSE polish phase.
    rem = max(0.0, t_end - time.monotonic() - 5.0)
    lse_budget = min(0.25 * budget, max(0.0, rem * 0.45))
    if lse_budget > 10.0:
        target = choose_target(best_score)
        a2, s2 = lse_polish(best_a, n, L, lse_budget, rng, target)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
            best_score = float(s2)
            best_a = a2

    # Remaining time: alternate, with exploratory noise when stuck.
    cycle = 0
    no_improve = 0
    while time.monotonic() < t_end - 5.0:
        remaining = t_end - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 15.0:
            break
        chunk = min(90.0, max(10.0, 0.25 * remaining))
        target = choose_target(best_score)

        if cycle % 2 == 0:
            a_loc, s_loc = sqrt_annel(best_a, n, L, chunk, rng, target)
        else:
            a_loc, s_loc = lse_polish(best_a, n, L, chunk, rng, target)

        if np.isfinite(s_loc) and s_loc < best_score:
            best_score = float(s_loc)
            best_a = a_loc
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 2 == 0:
                scale = 0.002 / np.sqrt(float(n))
                best_a = project_simplex(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, scale, n), 1.0)
        cycle += 1

    return best_a, best_score


def search_length(n, budget, rng, seed_seq=None):
    """Multi-start search at a fixed sequence length."""
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget))
    if budget < 5.0:
        a = init_binomial(n, False)
        c, _, s = eval_prob(a, next_pow2(2 * n - 1))
        return a, s

    n = int(n)
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    t_end = t0 + budget

    inits = make_inits(n, L, rng, seed_seq)
    scored = []
    for i, (name, arr) in enumerate(inits):
        arr = normalize_prob(arr)
        c, _, sc = eval_prob(arr, L)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            sc = 1e100
        scored.append((float(sc), i, arr, name))

    if len(scored) == 0:
        a = init_binomial(n, False)
        c, _, sc = eval_prob(a, L)
        return a, sc

    scored.sort()
    best_score = scored[0][0]
    best_a = scored[0][2].copy()

    # Quick refinement of the best few initializations.
    top_count = min(3, len(scored))
    quick_each = max(5.0, min(20.0, budget * 0.04))
    for i in range(top_count):
        sc0 = scored[i][0]
        a0 = scored[i][2]
        use_time = max(0.0, t_end - time.monotonic() - 5.0)
        use_time = min(quick_each, use_time)
        if use_time < 1.0:
            break
        target = choose_target(sc0)
        a_loc, s_loc = sqrt_annel(a0, n, L, use_time, rng, target)
        if np.isfinite(s_loc) and s_loc < best_score:
            best_score = float(s_loc)
            best_a = a_loc
            print("  [%d] quick init %s: %.6f" % (n, scored[i][3], best_score))

    rem = max(0.0, t_end - time.monotonic() - 8.0)
    if rem > 20.0:
        best_a, best_score = refine_sequence(best_a, n, L, rem, rng)
        print("  [%d] refined: %.6f" % (n, best_score))

    return best_a, best_score


def fallback_sequence():
    """Safe nonnegative fallback."""
    ref = get_reference_sequence()
    if ref is not None and 0 < ref.size <= 20000:
        return normalize_prob(ref)
    return init_binomial(4096, False)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2380.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Returns a nonnegative sequence of floats.  Runs up to budget_s seconds
    (clamped to 2380 to avoid violating external timeouts).
    """
    budget = min(float(budget_s), 2380.0)
    overall_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = overall_start + budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    global_best = np.inf
    global_a = None
    best8192 = None
    best8192_score = np.inf

    # Primary smaller length: faster, safer final objective evaluation.
    b1 = min(budget * 0.45, 1000.0)
    if b1 > 30.0:
        a1, s1 = search_length(8192, b1 - 20.0, rng, seed_seq=None)
        if a1 is not None and np.isfinite(s1):
            best8192 = a1
            best8192_score = float(s1)
            if s1 < global_best:
                global_best = float(s1)
                global_a = a1
        print("length 8192 best so far: %.6f" % global_best)

    # Try longer length, seeded by the best shorter result if available.
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 35.0)
    if rem > 180.0:
        seed_for_16k = global_a if global_best < np.inf else None
        a2, s2 = search_length(16384, rem, rng, seed_seq=seed_for_16k)
        if a2 is not None and np.isfinite(s2):
            if s2 < global_best:
                global_best = float(s2)
                global_a = a2
            print("length 16384 best so far: %.6f" % global_best)
    elif best8192 is None and rem > 40.0:
        # If the 8192 phase was tiny, at least produce a usable candidate.
        a3, s3 = search_length(4096, rem - 10.0, rng, seed_seq=None)
        if a3 is not None and np.isfinite(s3):
            global_best = float(s3)
            global_a = a3

    # If somehow no finite candidate, use a safe constructed fallback.
    if global_a is None or not np.isfinite(global_best):
        global_a = fallback_sequence()
        L = next_pow2(2 * global_a.size - 1)
        _, _, global_best = eval_prob(global_a, L)

    # Final sanitation.
    global_a = np.asarray(global_a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    global_a = np.clip(global_a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if global_a.size == 0 or float(np.sum(global_a)) < 0.01:
        global_a = np.full(4096, 1.0 / 4096.0, dtype=np.float64)

    # Do not spend remaining time verifying by the slow O(n^2) evaluator.
    # Internal FFT scores are used throughout; final values are valid nonnegative floats.
    if global_best < 1.5027:
        print("Final internal score: %.6f, length=%d" % (global_best, global_a.size))

    return [float(x) for x in global_a]
```
